## Objective
- Redo two-tree parity proof: `C:\RTR` is Role Type Retrieval alone, `C:\RTR-RE` is complete RTR-RE product; prove MCP/no-MCP parity per tree and shared behaviour across trees.

## Important Details
- Original todos source of truth: `C:\Users\Saimono\Desktop\Old-todos.md`; restored verbatim as 8 todos, pinned via `misrules_add`; TODO-1 in_progress, rest pending.
- Product distinction: RTR retrieval alone vs RTR-RE with Keeper, Symbiose context control, eviction, persistence, recall, installers, plugins; RTR-RE-only features are scope, not parity failures.
- Do not overwrite product identity via sync; preserve dirty worktree in `C:\RTR`; do not treat `C:\RTR-RE` as disposable mirror.
- Shell constraints: `bash` denies `head`, `rg`, `python*`, `grep`, `cat`, `find`, `sed`, `tail`, `awk`; use `^rg`, `^python`, `^git`, `^node` with nu syntax.
- Autonomous mode: keep going without stopping; gaps become listed assumptions.

## Work State
### Completed
- Restored 8 todos from `Old-todos.md` via `read`; verified no content diff; registered pin with `misrules_list` / `misrules_add`.
- Inventories: RTR 1156 files vs RE 624 files, 300 identical, 12 differing; `uv.lock` hash identical.
- Hashes verified: `pyproject.toml` identical `[NDATA]`; `memory_mcp_server.py` identical, `memory_cli.py` identical; `memory_engine.py` differs; `context_budget.py` differs; keeper differs `1fc35b...` vs `b90966...`.
- Surfaces: MCP `^def memory_` count 26 in both; CLI `add_parser` 15 parsers identical; `execution/` 24 entries RTR vs 20 RE; `rtr_presets.json` + `engine_beneath.py` exist only in RTR.
- Tests run: RTR `test_context_budget test_rtrre_matrix test_symbiose_worker test_rtr_presets test_engine_beneath test_architecture_contracts` etc: 102 tests, 1 error, 1 skipped; RE: 87 tests, 1 error, 6 skipped; error `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'chromadb'` in `test_mcp_status` via `memory_engine.py:27`.
- Keeper tests: RTR `opencode-plugin/rtr-re-keeper.test.mjs` 22 pass; RE 19 pass; RTR keeper `0.5.0` with `PRESET_REGISTRY`, `cpu`, `modality`, `threads`, `parking` verbs; RE keeper `0.4.1` with `320000/128000/40` defaults.
- mjs tests: `mjs/tests/rtr.test.mjs` 13 pass in both trees.
- Git state: `C:\RTR` `main...origin/main [ahead 3]` with 9 modified + 5 untracked (`rtr_presets.json`, `test_rtr_presets.py`, `engine_beneath.py`, `test_engine_beneath.py`, `uv.lock`).

### Active
- TODO-1 four-column matrix `RTR MCP / RTR no-MCP / RTR-RE MCP / RTR-RE no-MCP` in progress; collecting differing-file evidence, engine methods, env knobs, keeper verbs, directive bounds.

### Blocked
- `test_mcp_status` import error: `No module named 'chromadb'` in both trees.
- Shell denylist blocks bare `rg`, `python`, `head`, etc.; must use caret-prefixed externals.

## Next Move
1. Finish TODO-1 evidence matrix: compare shared retrieval vs RTR-only / RTR-RE-only vs measured defect across 12 differing files and 4 columns, then run identical scenarios per product family.
2. Verify identical `pyproject.toml` product-identity question before TODO-7; repair only measured defects with failing-first tests.

## Relevant Files
- `C:\Users\Saimono\Desktop\Old-todos.md`: original 8 todos, pinned rule file.
- `C:\RTR\pyproject.toml` / `C:\RTR-RE\pyproject.toml`: currently byte-identical, product-identity question.
- `C:\RTR\execution\context_budget.py` vs `C:\RTR-RE\execution\context_budget.py`: budget defaults differ (250k/100k preset lattice vs 320k/128k).
- `C:\RTR\execution\rtr_presets.json`: shared AH! lattice, only in RTR.
- `C:\RTR\execution\memory_engine.py` vs RE: discovery-scope fix diff.
- `C:\RTR\execution\memory_mcp_server.py`: 26 `memory_` tools, Keeper/Compressor/Manager status.
- `C:\RTR\execution\memory_cli.py`: CLI parsers surface.
- `C:\RTR\opencode-plugin\rtr-re-keeper.js` (0.5.0) vs `C:\RTR-RE\opencode-plugin\rtr-re-keeper.js` (0.4.1): preset/cpu/modality/threads/parking gap.
- `C:\RTR\directives\context-management.md`, `C:\RTR\directives\rtr-re-symbiose.md`: TRIGGER=320000 docs vs new preset defaults.
- `C:\RTR\eval\engine_beneath.py`, `C:\RTR\eval\TAXONOMY.md`: evaluation slices and corpora.
- `C:\RTR\.gitignore` vs `C:\RTR-RE\.gitignore`: lab vs standalone ignore rules.

Integrating real-time ctrl+p commands with existing slash commands for RTR-RE parameter control and keeper message limits while verifying deletion order.

Checking TUI plugin API and skill loader config to separate palette commands from server commands and protect todos.

Evaluating installer parity and registry dependency to avoid breaking the live plugin update.

Verifying path resolution for opaque deployment references and confirming config overrides versus hardcoded protections.

Analyzing RTR-RE architecture requirements for live ctrl+p parameter tuning and verifying keeper message deletion order.

Reconciling config paths and version differences between live plugin and repo sources.

Resolving RTR vs RTR-RE version mismatch and preset registry path conflicts before updating the live plugin.

Verifying config resolution and keeper eviction logic while updating the global plugin and tool protections.

Identifying the tool identifier for todos and adjusting the protected tools list while evaluating skill loading disablement.

Investigating how slash commands surface in the TUI palette and how server plugins can register them.

Separating live-tunable keeper knobs from static engine env vars for the palette design.

Drafting a temp test script to measure oldest-first deletion in the keeper algorithm.

Analyzing sync manifest and registry ownership to resolve keeper location and live update scope.

Evaluating lane authority and version drift to choose between direct live update and RTR-RE promotion.

[{ id: \"tp\", type: \"tool\", tool, state: { status: \"completed\", input: {}, output: text } }]\n    : [{ id: \"p1\", type: \"text\", text }];\n  return { info: { id: `m-${role}-${text.slice(0, 8)}`, role, sessionID: \"s1\" }, parts };\n}\nfunction run(label, server) {\n  const { planKeepIndices, resolveKeeperConfig } = server._test;\n  const cfg = resolveKeeperConfig({ triggerTokens: 500, targetTokens: 300, maxKeepMessages: 40 }, {});\n  const msgs = [];\n  for (let i = 0; i < 10; i++) msgs.push(msg(\"assistant\", `m${i}-` + \"x\".repeat(400)));\n  const plan = planKeepIndices(msgs, cfg);\n  console.log(`${label} keep=[${[...plan.keep].sort((a, b) => a - b)}] evict=[${plan.evict}] total=${plan.total} keepTokens=${plan.keepTokens}`);\n  const cfg2 = resolveKeeperConfig({ triggerTokens: 500, targetTokens: 300, maxKeepMessages: 40 }, {});\n  const msgs2 = [msg(\"assistant\", \"old-keep \" + \"y\".repeat(400), \"skill\")];\n  for (let i = 1; i < 9; i++) msgs2.push(msg(\"assistant\", `n${i}-` + \"x\".repeat(400)));\n  msgs2.push(msg(\"user\", \"latest \" + \"z\".repeat(400)));\n  const plan2 = planKeepIndices(msgs2, cfg2);\n  console.log(`${label}-protected keep=[${[...plan2.keep].sort((a, b) => a - b)}] evict=[${plan2.evict}]`);\n}\nrun(\"live-0.3.0\", live);\nrun(\"re-0.4.1\", re);\n","filePath":"C:\\Users\\Saimono\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\opencode\\keeper-oldest.mjs"} Wrote file successfully.

You're right — less mapping, more doing. Quick answer on the replacement, then I'm making the changes.

`rtr-memory.js` (shell to `rtr-cli` on chat/compact) wasn't replaced by one file. Its auto-persist job is now the keeper core itself — `rtr-re-keeper.js` transform + sink to `directives/sessions/` on every request. `rtr-core.js` is the no-MCP host bridge (rules, optimizer, CLI args), and `memory_engine.py` is the Python manager underneath both. So: keeper replaced the hook, core covers other hosts, engine stayed the manager.

Now acting: protection switch, 0.5.0 sync, live update, skills off.

## span 62
role: assistant
lane: read
dtype: CDATA


You're right — less mapping, more doing. Quick answer on the replacement, then I'm making the changes.

`rtr-memory.js` (shell to `rtr-cli` on chat/compact) wasn't replaced by one file. Its auto-persist job is now the keeper core itself — `rtr-re-keeper.js` transform + sink to `directives/sessions/` on every request. `rtr-core.js` is the no-MCP host bridge (rules, optimizer, CLI args), and `memory_engine.py` is the Python manager underneath both. So: keeper replaced the hook, core covers other hosts, engine stayed the manager.

Now acting: protection switch, 0.5.0 sync, live update, skills off.

Summarizing the RTR-RE architecture update, version 0.

Updating plugin paths and version to 0.5.0 while adjusting protected config and disabling legacy skill loading.

Designing a TUI plugin companion to expose keeper controls in the command palette alongside slash commands.

Evaluating sync_standalone.py against the manifest to avoid deleting the keeper config directory.

Extending the sync plan to copy only keeper files and presets while updating protection settings and disabling skill loading.

Extending filtering to block skill tool execution via permission deny while noting discovery depends on empty skill directories.

Shifting from TUI plugin feasibility to completing the server-side RTR switch and syncing the surgical set.

Finalizing the RTR-RE sync and live plugin update sequence with backup and permission changes.

Sequencing protectedTools edits and selecting uv for python tests under the deny pattern.

Staging two sequential edit batches and verifying the sync command path.


Protection switch starts now — source first, then sync, then live.

 76: Local plugins and custom tools can use external npm packages. Add a `package.json` to your config directory with the dependencies you need.

 74: ### Dependencies
 73: 
 72: ---

 69: A plugin is a **JavaScript/TypeScript module** that exports one or more plugin
 68: 
 67: ## Create a plugin
 66: 
 65: ---
 64: 
 63: Duplicate npm packages with the same name and version are loaded once. However, a local plugin and an npm plugin with similar names are both loaded separately.
 62: 
 61: 4. Project plugin directory (`.opencode/plugins/`)
 60: 3. Global plugin directory (`~/.config/opencode/plugins/`)
 59: 2. Project config (`opencode.json`)
 58: 1. Global config (`~/.config/opencode/opencode.json`)
 57: 
 56: Plugins are loaded from all sources and all hooks run in sequence. The load order is:
 55: 
 54: ### Load order
 53: 
 52: ---
 51: 
 50: **Local plugins** are loaded directly from the plugin directory. To use external packages, you must create a `package.json` within your config directory (see [Dependencies](#dependencies)), or publish the plugin to npm and [add it to your config](/docs/config#plugins).
 49: 
 48: **npm plugins** are installed automatically using Bun at startup. Packages and their dependencies are cached in `~/.cache/opencode/node_modules/`.
 47: 
 46: ### How plugins are installed
 45: 
 44: ---
 43: 
 42: Browse available plugins in the [ecosystem](/docs/ecosystem#plugins).
 41: 
 40: Both regular and scoped npm packages are supported.

 37: }

 34: {

 32: 
 31: Specify npm packages in your config file.
 30: 
 29: ### From npm
 28: 
 27: ---
 26: 
 25: Files in these directories are automatically loaded at startup.
 24: 
 23: - `~/.config/opencode/plugins/` - Global plugins
 22: - `.opencode/plugins/` - Project-level plugins
 21: 
 20: Place JavaScript or TypeScript files in the plugin directory.
 19: 
 18: ### From local files
 17: 
 16: ---
 15: 
 14: There are two ways to load plugins.
 13: 
 12: ## Use a plugin

 10: ---
  9:

908: ### Experimental
907: 
906: ---
905: 
904: If a provider appears in both `enabled_providers` and `disabled_providers`, the `disabled_providers` takes priority for backwards compatibility.
903: 
902: :::
901: The `disabled_providers` takes priority over `enabled_providers`.
900: :::note
899: 
898: This is useful when you want to restrict OpenCode to only use specific providers rather than disabling them one by one.
897: 
896: ```
895: }
894:   "enabled_providers": ["anthropic", "openai"]
893:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
892: {
891: ```json title="opencode.json"
890: 
889: You can specify an allowlist of providers through the `enabled_providers` option. When set, only the specified providers will be enabled and all others will be ignored.
888: 
887: ### Enabled providers
886: 
885: ---
884: 
883: - The provider's models won't appear in the model selection list.
882: - It won't be loaded even if API keys are configured through the `/connect` command.
881: - It won't be loaded even if environment variables are set.
880: 
879: The `disabled_providers` option accepts an array of provider IDs. When a provider is disabled:
878: 
877: :::
876: The `disabled_providers` takes priority over `enabled_providers`.
875: :::note
874: 
873: ```
872: }
871:   "disabled_providers": ["openai", "gemini"]
870:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
869: {
868: ```json title="opencode.json"
867: 
866: You can disable providers that are loaded automatically through the `disabled_providers` option. This is useful when you want to prevent certain providers from being loaded even if their credentials are available.
865: 
864: ### Disabled providers
863: 
862: ---
861: 
860: about rules here](/docs/rules).
859: This takes an array of paths and glob patterns to instruction files. [Learn more
858: 
857: ```
856: }
855:   "instructions": ["CONTRIBUTING.md", "docs/guidelines.md", ".cursor/rules/*.md"]
854:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
853: {
852: ```json title="opencode.json"
851: 
850: You can configure the instructions for the model you're using through the `instructions` option.
849: 
848: ### Instructions
847: 
846: ---
845: 
844: [Learn more here](/docs/plugins).
843: 
842: ```
841: }
840:   "plugin": ["opencode-helicone-session", "@my-org/custom-plugin"]
839:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
838: {
837: ```json title="opencode.json"
836: 
835: Place plugin files in `.opencode/plugins/` or `~/.config/opencode/plugins/`. You can also load plugins from npm through the `plugin` option.
834: 
833: [Plugins](/docs/plugins) extend OpenCode with custom tools, hooks, and integrations.
832: 
831: ### Plugins
830: 
829: ---
828:

826: 
825: best-effort and can never break a session.
824: `memory` is connected; set `enabled: false` to opt out. All calls are
823: Omit `memory` entirely and it still activates whenever an MCP server named
822:

820: }
819:   }
818:     "max_chars": 4000
817:     "timeout": 120000,
816:     "commit_on_compact": true,
815:     "restore_on_start": true,
814:     "server": "memory",
813:     "enabled": true,
812:   "memory": {
811:   },
810:     }
809:       "command": ["uvx", "--from", "rtr-role-type-retrieval", "rtr-mcp"]
808:       "type": "local",
807:     "memory": {

803: ```json title="opencode.json"
802: 
801: through a memory MCP server (e.g. RTR). No prompts, no rules needed.
800: Automatic project memory restore on session start and commit on compaction,
799: 
798: ### Memory
797: 
796: ---
795: 
794: [Learn more here](/docs/mcp-servers).
793: 
792: ```
791: }
790:   "mcp": {}
789:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
788: {
787: ```json title="opencode.json"
786: 
785: You can configure MCP servers you want to use through the `mcp` option.
784: 
783: ### MCP servers
782: 
781: ---
780: 
779: Patterns follow glob syntax. Use this to exclude noisy directories from file watching.
778: 
777: ```
776: }
775:   }
774:     "ignore": ["node_modules/**", "dist/**", ".git/**"]
773:   "watcher": {
772:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
771: {
770: ```json title="opencode.json"
769: 
768: You can configure file watcher ignore patterns through the `watcher` option.
767: 
766: ### Watcher
765: 
764: ---
763: 
762: - `reserved` - Token buffer for compaction. Leaves enough window to avoid overflow during compaction.
761: - `prune` - Remove old tool outputs to save tokens (default: `false`). Set to `true` to enable pruning.
760: - `auto` - Automatically compact the session when context is full (default: `true`).
759: 
758: ```
757: }
756:   }
755:     "reserved": 10000
754:     "prune": false,
753:     "auto": true,
752:   "compaction": {
751:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
750: {
749: ```json title="opencode.json"
748: 
747: You can control context compaction behavior through the `compaction` option.
746: 
745: ### Compaction
744: 
743: ---
742: 
741: [Learn more about permissions here](/docs/permissions).
740: 
739: ```
738: }
737:   }
736:     "bash": "ask"
735:     "edit": "ask",
734:   "permission": {
733:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
732: {
731: ```json title="opencode.json"
730: 
729: For example, to ensure that the `edit` and `bash` tools require user approval:
728: 
727: By default, opencode **allows all operations** without requiring explicit approval. You can change this using the `permission` option.
726: 
725: ### Permissions
724: 
723: ---
722: 
721: [Learn more about LSP servers here](/docs/lsp).
720: 
719: ```
718: }
717:   }
716:     }
715:       "disabled": true
714:     "typescript": {
713:   "lsp": {
712:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
711: {
710: ```json title="opencode.json"
709: 
708: Use an object to keep built-ins enabled while configuring overrides or custom LSP servers.
707: 
706: ```
705: }
704:   "lsp": true
703:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
702: {
701: ```json title="opencode.json"
700: 
699: You can enable and configure LSP servers through the `lsp` option. Omit it to keep LSP disabled.
698: 
697: ### LSP Servers
696: 
695: ---
694: 
693: [Learn more about formatters here](/docs/formatters).
692: 
691: ```
690: }
689:   }
688:     }
687:       "extensions": [".js", ".ts", ".jsx", ".tsx"]
686:       },
685:         "NODE_ENV": "development"
684:       "environment": {
683:       "command": ["npx", "prettier", "--write", "$FILE"],
682:     "custom-prettier": {
681:     },
680:       "disabled": true
679:     "prettier": {
678:   "formatter": {
677:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
676: {
675: ```json title="opencode.json"
674: 
673: Use an object to keep built-ins enabled while configuring overrides or custom formatters.
672: 
671: ```
670: }
669:   "formatter": true
668:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
667: {
666: ```json title="opencode.json"
665: 
664: You can enable and configure code formatters through the `formatter` option. Omit it to keep formatters disabled.
663: 
662: ### Formatters
661: 
660: ---
659: 
658: Notice that this only works if it was not installed using a package manager such as Homebrew.
657: If you don't want updates but want to be notified when a new version is available, set `autoupdate` to `"notify"`.
656: 
655: ```
654: }
653:   "autoupdate": false
652:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
651: {
650: ```json title="opencode.json"
649: 
648: OpenCode will automatically download any new updates when it starts up. You can disable this with the `autoupdate` option.
647: 
646: ### Autoupdate
645: 
644: ---
643: 
642: Note that disabling snapshots means changes made by the agent cannot be rolled back through the UI.
641: 
640: ```
639: }
638:   "snapshot": false
637:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
636: {
635: ```json title="opencode.json"
634: 
633: For large repositories or projects with many submodules, the snapshot system can cause slow indexing and significant disk usage as it tracks all changes using an internal git repository. You can disable snapshots using the `snapshot` option.
632: 
631: OpenCode uses snapshots to track file changes during agent operations, enabling you to undo and revert changes within a session. Snapshots are enabled by default.
630: 
629: ### Snapshot
628: 
627: ---
626: 
625: [Learn more here](/docs/keybinds).
624: 
623: `keybinds` is merged with built-in defaults, so you only need to configure the shortcuts you want to change.
622: 
621: ```
620: }
619:   }
618:     "command_list": "ctrl+p"
617:   "keybinds": {
616:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/tui.json",
615: {
614: ```json title="tui.json"
613: 
612: Customize TUI keyboard shortcuts in `tui.json` with `keybinds`.
611: 
610: ### Keybinds
609: 
608: ---
607: 
606: You can also define commands using markdown files in `~/.config/opencode/commands/` or `.opencode/commands/`. [Learn more here](/docs/commands).
605: 
604: ```
603: }
602:   },
601:     },
600:       "description": "Create a new component",
599:       "template": "Create a new React component named $ARGUMENTS with TypeScript support.\nInclude proper typing and basic structure.",
598:     "component": {
597:     },
596:       "model": "anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5",
595:       "agent": "build",
594:       "description": "Run tests with coverage",
593:       "template": "Run the full test suite with coverage report and show any failures.\nFocus on the failing tests and suggest fixes.",
592:     "test": {
591:   "command": {
590:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
589: {
588: ```jsonc title="opencode.jsonc"
587: 
586: You can configure custom commands for repetitive tasks through the `command` option.
585: 
584: ### Commands
583: 
582: ---
581: 
580: By default, sharing is set to manual mode where you need to explicitly share conversations using the `/share` command.
579: 
578: - `"disabled"` - Disable sharing entirely
577: - `"auto"` - Automatically share new conversations
576: - `"manual"` - Allow manual sharing via commands (default)
575: 
574: This takes:
573: 
572: ```
571: }
570:   "share": "manual"
569:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
568: {
567: ```json title="opencode.json"
566: 
565: You can configure the [share](/docs/share) feature through the `share` option.
564: 
563: ### Sharing
562: 
561: ---
560: 
559: The default is `1`, which allows primary agents to launch subagents but prevents those subagents from launching additional subagents. Set it to `2` to allow one additional level of nested subagents, or `0` to prevent all subagent launches.
558: 
557: ```
556: }
555:   "subagent_depth": 2
554:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
553: {
552: ```json title="opencode.json"
551: 
550: You can control how deeply subagents can invoke other subagents using the `subagent_depth` option.
549: 
548: ### Subagent depth
547: 
546: ---
545: 
544: This setting applies across all interfaces: TUI, CLI (`opencode run`), desktop app, and GitHub Action.
543: 
542: The default agent must be a primary agent (not a subagent). This can be a built-in agent like `"build"` or `"plan"`, or a [custom agent](/docs/agents) you've defined. If the specified agent doesn't exist or is a subagent, OpenCode will fall back to `"build"` with a warning.
541: 
540: ```
539: }
538:   "default_agent": "plan"
537:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
536: {
535: ```json title="opencode.json"
534: 
533: You can set the default agent using the `default_agent` option. This determines which agent is used when none is explicitly specified.
532: 
531: ### Default agent
530: 
529: ---
528: 
527: You can also define agents using markdown files in `~/.config/opencode/agents/` or `.opencode/agents/`. [Learn more here](/docs/agents).
526: 
525: ```
524: }
523:   },
522:     },
521:       },
520:         "edit": false,
519:         "write": false,
518:         // Disable file modification tools for review-only agent
517:       "tools": {
516:       "prompt": "You are a code reviewer. Focus on security, performance, and maintainability.",
515:       "model": "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-5",
514:       "description": "Reviews code for best practices and potential issues",
513:     "code-reviewer": {
512:   "agent": {
511:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
510: {
509: ```jsonc title="opencode.jsonc"
508: 
507: You can configure specialized agents for specific tasks through the `agent` option.
506: 
505: ### Agents
504: 
503: ---
502: 
501: [Learn more here](/docs/themes).
500: 
499: ```
498: }
497:   "theme": "tokyonight"
496:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/tui.json",
495: {
494: ```json title="tui.json"
493: 
492: Set your UI theme in `tui.json`.
491: 
490: ### Themes
489: 
488: ---
487: 
486: [Learn more about Amazon Bedrock configuration](/docs/providers#amazon-bedrock).
485: 
484: :::
483: Bearer tokens (`AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` or `/connect`) take precedence over profile-based authentication. See [authentication precedence](/docs/providers#authentication-precedence) for details.
482: :::note
481: 
480: - `endpoint` - Custom endpoint URL for VPC endpoints. This is an alias for the generic `baseURL` option using AWS-specific terminology. If both are specified, `endpoint` takes precedence.
479: - `profile` - AWS named profile from `~/.aws/credentials` (defaults to `AWS_PROFILE` env var)
478: - `region` - AWS region for Bedrock (defaults to `AWS_REGION` env var or `us-east-1`)
477: 
476: ```
475: }
474:   }
473:     }
472:       }
471:         "endpoint": "https://bedrock-runtime.us-east-1.vpce-xxxxx.amazonaws.com"
470:         "profile": "my-aws-profile",
469:         "region": "us-east-1",
468:       "options": {
467:     "amazon-bedrock": {
466:   "provider": {
465:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
464: {
463: ```json title="opencode.json"
462: 
461: Amazon Bedrock supports AWS-specific configuration:
460: 
459: ##### Amazon Bedrock
458: 
457: Some providers support additional configuration options beyond the generic `timeout` and `apiKey` settings.
456: 
455: #### Provider-Specific Options
454: 
453: ---
452: 
451: If an image still cannot fit after resizing, OpenCode omits oversized tool-result images or fails oversized user-provided images with an image size error.
450: 
449: - `max_base64_bytes` - Maximum encoded image payload size. This is the base64 payload size, not the original file size.
448: - `max_height` - Maximum image height in pixels before resizing or rejection.
447: - `max_width` - Maximum image width in pixels before resizing or rejection.
446: - `auto_resize` - Resize images that exceed the configured limits before provider requests. Set to `false` to reject oversized images instead.
445: 
444: ```
443: }
442:   }
441:     }
440:       "max_base64_bytes": 5242880
439:       "max_height": 2000,
438:       "max_width": 2000,
437:       "auto_resize": true,
436:     "image": {
435:   "attachment": {
434:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
433: {
432: ```json title="opencode.json"
431: 
430: Configure image attachment limits with the `attachment.image` option:
429: 
428: OpenCode normalizes image attachments before sending them to the model. By default, images are resized when they exceed `2000x2000` pixels or `5242880` base64 bytes.
427: 
426: ### Image attachments
425: 
424: ---
423: 
422: [Learn more about policies here](/docs/policies).
421: 
420: ```
419: }
418:   }
417:     ]
416:       }
415:         "resource": "openai"
414:         "action": "provider.use",
413:         "effect": "deny",
412:       {
411:     "policies": [
410:   "experimental": {
409:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
408: {
407: ```json title="opencode.json"
406: 
405: Use the `experimental.policies` option to allow or deny OpenCode actions on configured resources. Currently, policies can control which providers OpenCode may use.
404: 
403: ### Policies
402: 
401: ---
400: 
399: You can also configure [local models](/docs/models#local). [Learn more](/docs/models).
398: 
397: - `setCacheKey` - Ensure a cache key is always set for designated provider.
396: - `chunkTimeout` - Timeout in milliseconds between streamed response chunks (default: 300000, or 5 minutes). If no chunk arrives in time, the request is aborted. Set to `false` to disable.
395: - `headerTimeout` - Timeout in milliseconds to wait for response headers (default: 300000, or 5 minutes). This timer stops once headers arrive and does not limit the streamed response body. Set to `false` to disable.
394: - `timeout` - Request timeout in milliseconds (default: 300000). Set to `false` to disable.
393: 
392: ```
391: }
390:   }
389:     }
388:       }
387:         "setCacheKey": true
386:         "chunkTimeout": 30000,
385:         "timeout": 600000,
384:       "options": {
383:     "anthropic": {
382:   "provider": {
381:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
380: {
379: ```json title="opencode.json"
378: 
377: Provider options can include `timeout`, `headerTimeout`, `chunkTimeout`, and `setCacheKey`:
376: 
375: The `small_model` option configures a separate model for lightweight tasks like title generation. By default, OpenCode tries to use a cheaper model if one is available from your provider, otherwise it falls back to your main model.
374: 
373: ```
372: }
371:   "small_model": "anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5"
370:   "model": "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-5",
369:   "provider": {},
368:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
367: {
366: ```json title="opencode.json"
365: 
364: You can configure the providers and models you want to use in your OpenCode config through the `provider`, `model` and `small_model` options.
363: 
362: ### Models
361: 
360: ---
359: 
358: [Learn more about tools here](/docs/tools).
357: 
356: ```
355: }
354:   }
353:     "bash": false
352:     "write": false,
351:   "tools": {
350:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
349: {
348: ```json title="opencode.json"
347: 
346: You can manage the tools an LLM can use through the `tools` option.
345: 
344: ### Tools
343: 
342: ---
341: 
340: If not specified, OpenCode will automatically discover and use a sensible default based on your operating system (e.g. `pwsh` or `cmd.exe` on Windows, `/bin/zsh` or `/bin/bash` on macOS/Linux). You can provide an absolute path or a short name.
339: 
338: ```
337: }
336:   "shell": "pwsh"
335:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
334: {
333: ```json title="opencode.json"
332: 
331: You can configure the shell used for the interactive terminal using the `shell` option. Compatible shells are also used for agent tool calls.
330: 
329: ### Shell
328: 
327: ---
326: 
325: [Learn more about the server here](/docs/server).
324: 
323: - `cors` - Additional origins to allow for CORS when using the HTTP server from a browser-based client. Values must be full origins (scheme + host + optional port), eg `https://app.example.com`.
322: - `mdnsDomain` - Custom domain name for mDNS service. Defaults to `opencode.local`. Useful for running multiple instances on the same network.
321: - `mdns` - Enable mDNS service discovery. This allows other devices on the network to discover your OpenCode server.
320: - `hostname` - Hostname to listen on. When `mdns` is enabled and no hostname is set, defaults to `0.0.0.0`.
319: - `port` - Port to listen on.
318: 
317: Available options:
316: 
315: ```
314: }
313:   }
312:     "cors": ["http://localhost:5173"]
311:     "mdnsDomain": "myproject.local",
310:     "mdns": true,
309:     "hostname": "0.0.0.0",
308:     "port": 4096,
307:   "server": {
306:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
305: {
304: ```json title="opencode.json"
303: 
302: You can configure server settings for the `opencode serve` and `opencode web` commands through the `server` option.
301: 
300: ### Server
299: 
298: ---
297: 
296: Legacy `theme`, `keybinds`, and `tui` keys in `opencode.json` are deprecated and automatically migrated when possible.
295: 
294: Set `attention.enabled` to turn on TUI desktop notifications and sounds. See [TUI attention](/docs/tui#attention).
293: 
292: When `cursor.style` is `"default"`, the terminal default cursor is restored, so `cursor.blinking` has no effect.
291: 
290: Use `OPENCODE_TUI_CONFIG` to point to a custom TUI config file.
289: 
288: ```
287: }
286:   }
285:     "volume": 0.4
284:     "sound": true,
283:     "notifications": true,
282:     "enabled": true,
281:   "attention": {
280:   "mouse": true,
279:   },
278:     "blinking": true
277:     "style": "block",
276:   "cursor": {
275:   "diff_style": "auto",
274:   },
273:     "enabled": true
272:   "scroll_acceleration": {
271:   "scroll_speed": 3,
270:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/tui.json",
269: {
268: ```json title="tui.json"
267: 
266: Use a dedicated `tui.json` (or `tui.jsonc`) file for TUI-specific settings.
265: 
264: ### TUI
263: 
262: ---
261: 
260: Your editor should be able to validate and autocomplete based on the schema.
259: 
258: TUI config uses [**`opencode.ai/tui.json`**](https://opencode.ai/tui.json).
257: 
256: The server/runtime config schema is defined in [**`opencode.ai/config.json`**](https://opencode.ai/config.json).
255: 
254: ## Schema
253: 
252: ---
251: 
250: All managed preference keys appear in the resolved config and cannot be overridden by user or project configuration.
249: 
248: ```
247: opencode debug config
246: ```bash
245: 
244: Double-click the `.mobileconfig` to install locally for testing (shows in System Settings > Privacy & Security > Profiles), then run:
243: 
242: **Verifying on a device**
241: 
240: - **FleetDM:** Add the `.mobileconfig` to your gitops repo under `mdm.macos_settings.custom_settings` and run `fleetctl apply`
239: - **Jamf Pro:** Computers > Configuration Profiles > Upload > scope to target devices or smart groups
238: 
237: **Deploying via MDM**
236: 
235: Generate unique UUIDs with `uuidgen`. Customize the settings to match your organization's requirements.
234: 
233: ```
232: </plist>
231: </dict>
230:   <integer>1</integer>
229:   <key>PayloadVersion</key>
228:   <string>GENERATE-YOUR-OWN-UUID</string>
227:   <key>PayloadUUID</key>
226:   <string>com.example.opencode</string>
225:   <key>PayloadIdentifier</key>
224:   <string>Configuration</string>
223:   <key>PayloadType</key>
222:   </array>
221:     </dict>
220:       </dict>
219:         </dict>
218:           <string>deny</string>
217:           <key>rm -rf *</key>
216:           <string>ask</string>
215:           <key>*</key>
214:         <dict>
213:         <key>bash</key>
212:         <string>ask</string>
211:         <key>*</key>
210:       <dict>
209:       <key>permission</key>
208:       </dict>
207:         <string>127.0.0.1</string>
206:         <key>hostname</key>
205:       <dict>
204:       <key>server</key>
203:       <string>disabled</string>
202:       <key>share</key>
201:       <integer>1</integer>
200:       <key>PayloadVersion</key>
199:       <string>GENERATE-YOUR-OWN-UUID</string>
198:       <key>PayloadUUID</key>
197:       <string>com.example.opencode.config</string>
196:       <key>PayloadIdentifier</key>
195:       <string>ai.opencode.managed</string>

193:     <dict>
192:   <array>

186: <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
185: ```xml
184: 
183: Use the `ai.opencode.managed` PayloadType. The OpenCode config keys go directly in the payload dict:
182: 
181: **Creating a `.mobileconfig`**
180: 
179: The plist keys map directly to `opencode.json` fields. MDM metadata keys (`PayloadUUID`, `PayloadType`, etc.) are stripped automatically.
178: 
177: 2. `/Library/Managed Preferences/ai.opencode.managed.plist`
176: 1. `/Library/Managed Preferences/<user>/ai.opencode.managed.plist`
175: 
174: OpenCode checks these paths:
173: 
172: On macOS, OpenCode reads managed preferences from the `ai.opencode.managed` preference domain. Deploy a `.mobileconfig` via MDM (Jamf, Kandji, FleetDM) and the settings are enforced automatically.
171: 
170: #### macOS managed preferences
169: 
168: These directories require admin/root access to write, so users cannot modify them.
167: 
166: | Windows  | `%ProgramData%\opencode`                 |
165: | Linux    | `/etc/opencode/`                         |
164: | macOS    | `/Library/Application Support/opencode/` |
163: | -------- | ---------------------------------------- |
162: | Platform | Path                                     |
161: 
160: Drop an `opencode.json` or `opencode.jsonc` file in the system managed config directory:
159: 
158: #### File-based
157: 
156: Organizations can enforce configuration that users cannot override. Managed settings are loaded at the highest priority tier.
155: 
154: ### Managed settings
153: 
152: ---
151: 
150: The custom directory is loaded after the global config and `.opencode` directories, so it **can override** their settings.
149: 
148: ```
147: opencode run "Hello world"
146: export OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR=/path/to/my/config-directory
145: ```bash
144: 
143: follow the same structure.
142: modes, and plugins just like the standard `.opencode` directory, and should
141: environment variable. This directory will be searched for agents, commands,
140: Specify a custom config directory using the `OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR`
139: 
138: ### Custom directory
137: 
136: ---
135: 
134: Custom config is loaded between global and project configs in the precedence order.
133: 
132: ```
131: opencode run "Hello world"
130: export OPENCODE_CONFIG=/path/to/my/custom-config.json
129: ```bash
128: 
127: Specify a custom config file path using the `OPENCODE_CONFIG` environment variable.
126: 
125: ### Custom path
124: 
123: ---
122: 
121: This is also safe to be checked into Git and uses the same schema as the global one.
120: 
119: When OpenCode starts up, it first looks for a config file in the current directory, then traverses up to the nearest Git directory.
118: 
117: :::
116: Place project specific config in the root of your project.
115: :::tip
114: 
113: For project-specific TUI settings, add `tui.json` alongside it.
112: 
111: Add `opencode.json` in your project root. Project config has the highest precedence among standard config files - it overrides both global and remote configs.
110: 
109: ### Per project
108: 
107: ---
106: 
105: Global config overrides remote organizational defaults.
104: 
103: For TUI-specific settings, use `~/.config/opencode/tui.json`.
102: 
101: Place your global OpenCode config in `~/.config/opencode/opencode.json`. Use global config for user-wide server/runtime preferences like providers, models, and permissions.
100: 
 99: ### Global
 98: 
 97: ---
 96: 
 95: ```
 94: }
 93:   }
 92:     }
 91:       "enabled": true
 90:       "url": "https://jira.example.com/mcp",
 89:       "type": "remote",
 88:     "jira": {
 87:   "mcp": {
 86: {
 85: ```json title="opencode.json"
 84: 
 83: You can enable specific servers in your local config:
 82: 
 81: ```
 80: }
 79:   }
 78:     }
 77:       "enabled": false
 76:       "url": "https://jira.example.com/mcp",
 75:       "type": "remote",
 74:     "jira": {
 73:   "mcp": {
 72: {
 71: ```json title="Remote config from .well-known/opencode"
 70: 
 69: For example, if your organization provides MCP servers that are disabled by default:
 68: 
 67: Remote config is loaded first, serving as the base layer. All other config sources (global, project) can override these defaults.
 66: 
 65: Organizations can provide default configuration via the `.well-known/opencode` endpoint. This is fetched automatically when you authenticate with a provider that supports it.
 64: 
 63: ### Remote
 62: 
 61: ---
 60: 
 59: :::
 58: The `.opencode` and `~/.config/opencode` directories use **plural names** for subdirectories: `agents/`, `commands/`, `modes/`, `plugins/`, `skills/`, `tools/`, and `themes/`. Singular names (e.g., `agent/`) are also supported for backwards compatibility.
 57: :::note
 56: 
 55: This means project configs can override global defaults, and global configs can override remote organizational defaults. Managed settings override everything.
 54: 
 53: 8. **macOS managed preferences** (`.mobileconfig` via MDM) - highest priority, not user-overridable
 52: 7. **Managed config files** (`/Library/Application Support/opencode/` on macOS) - admin-controlled
 51: 6. **Inline config** (`OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` env var) - runtime overrides
 50: 5. **`.opencode` directories** - agents, commands, plugins
 49: 4. **Project config** (`opencode.json` in project) - project-specific settings
 48: 3. **Custom config** (`OPENCODE_CONFIG` env var) - custom overrides
 47: 2. **Global config** (`~/.config/opencode/opencode.json`) - user preferences
 46: 1. **Remote config** (from `.well-known/opencode`) - organizational defaults
 45: 
 44: Config sources are loaded in this order (later sources override earlier ones):
 43: 
 42: ### Precedence order
 41: 
 40: ---
 39: 
 38: For example, if your global config sets `autoupdate: true` and your project config sets `model: "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-5"`, the final configuration will include both settings.
 37: 
 36: Configuration files are merged together, not replaced. Settings from the following config locations are combined. Later configs override earlier ones only for conflicting keys. Non-conflicting settings from all configs are preserved.
 35: 
 34: :::
 33: Configuration files are **merged together**, not replaced.
 32: :::note
 31: 
 30: different order of precedence.
 29: You can place your config in a couple of different locations and they have a
 28: 
 27: ## Locations
 26: 
 25: ---
 24: 
 23: ```
 22: }
 21:   },
 20:     "port": 4096,
 19:   "server": {
 18:   "autoupdate": true,
 17:   "model": "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-5",
 16:   "$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json",
 15: {
 14: ```jsonc title="opencode.jsonc"
 13: 
 12: OpenCode supports both **JSON** and **JSONC** (JSON with Comments) formats.
 11: 
 10: ## Format
  9: 
  8: ---
  7: 
  6: You can configure OpenCode using a JSON config file.

You're right — less mapping, more doing. Quick answer on the replacement, then I'm making the changes.

`rtr-memory.js` wasn't replaced by one file. Its auto-persist job is now the keeper core itself — transform + sink on every request. `rtr-core.js` is the no-MCP host bridge, `memory_engine.py` the manager underneath. So: keeper replaced the hook.

Protection is now todos-only in source, tests green. Finishing the sync recovery and live update now.

write {"content":"# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Nova-Violet Role ORG\n# SPDX-License-Identifier: EUPL-1.2 OR AGPL-3.0-or-later\nimport importlib.util\nimport json\nimport tempfile\nimport unittest\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).parent.parent\nSPEC = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(\n    \"sync_standalone\", ROOT / \"tools\" / \"sync_standalone.py\"\n)\nassert SPEC is not None and SPEC.loader is not None\nsync_standalone = importlib.util.module_from_spec(SPEC)\nSPEC.loader.exec_module(sync_standalone)\n\n\nclass TestSyncStandalone(unittest.TestCase):\n    def test_sync_excludes_vendored_and_preserves_local_build_outputs(self):\n        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as td:\n            dst = Path(td) / \"standalone\"\n            self.assertEqual(sync_standalone.main([\"--dst\", str(dst)]), 0)\n            manifest = json.loads(\n                (dst / \"SYNC-MANIFEST.json\").read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n            )\n            paths = [entry[\"path\"] for entry in manifest[\"files\"]]\n            self.assertTrue(paths)\n            self.assertFalse(any(p.startswith(\"rolling-context/\") for p in paths))\n            self.assertFalse(any(p.startswith(\"opencode-acp/\") for p in paths))\n            self.assertIn(\"opencode-plugin/rtr-re-keeper.js\", paths)\n            self.assertIn(\"uv.lock\", paths)\n            self.assertIn(\"tui-plugin/rtr-keeper-palette.ts\", paths)\n            self.assertIn(\"mjs/.opencode/plugins/rtr.mjs\", paths)\n            self.assertIn(\"mjs/hooks/rtr-core.js\", paths)\n            self.assertIn(\"mjs/tests/rtr.test.mjs\", paths)\n            self.assertIn(\"tools/sync_standalone.py\", paths)\n            self.assertIn(\"tools/test_sync_standalone.py\", paths)\n\n            wheel_sentinel = dst / \"dist\" / \"keep.whl\"\n            lake_sentinel = dst / \"formal\" / \".lake\" / \"keep.txt\"\n            stale = dst / \"execution\" / \"stale-source.py\"\n            opencode_sentinel = dst / \".opencode\" / \"rtr-re-keeper.json\"\n            coco_sentinel = dst / \".cocoindex_code\" / \"keep.db\"\n            wheel_sentinel.parent.mkdir(parents=True)\n            lake_sentinel.parent.mkdir(parents=True)\n            opencode_sentinel.parent.mkdir(parents=True)\n            coco_sentinel.parent.mkdir(parents=True)\n            wheel_sentinel.write_bytes(b\"wheel\")\n            lake_sentinel.write_bytes(b\"lake\")\n            stale.write_bytes(b\"stale\")\n            opencode_sentinel.write_bytes(b\"live\")\n            coco_sentinel.write_bytes(b\"index\")\n\n            self.assertEqual(sync_standalone.main([\"--dst\", str(dst)]), 0)\n            self.assertTrue(wheel_sentinel.exists())\n            self.assertTrue(lake_sentinel.exists())\n            self.assertTrue(opencode_sentinel.exists())\n            self.assertTrue(coco_sentinel.exists())\n            self.assertFalse(stale.exists())\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    unittest.main()\n","filePath":"C:\\RTR\\tools\\test_sync_standalone.py"} Wrote file successfully.

(!cond) fails++;\n}\nfunction loadJsonc(path) {\n  const raw = fs.readFileSync(path, \"utf8\");\n  const stripped = raw.split(\"\\n\").filter((l) => !l.trim().startsWith(\"//\")).join(\"\\n\");\n  return { raw, json: JSON.parse(stripped) };\n}\n\n// opencode.jsonc: skills denied exactly once per scope, keeper wired, memory on\n{\n  const { raw, json } = loadJsonc(\"C:/Users/Saimono/.config/opencode/opencode.jsonc\");\n  check(\"global skill deny\", json.permission.skill, \"deny\");\n  check(\"muse-code skill deny\", json.agent[\"muse-code\"].permission.skill, \"deny\");\n  check(\"muse-wonder skill deny\", json.agent[\"muse-wonder\"].permission.skill, \"deny\");\n  const count = (raw.match(/\"skill\": \"deny\"/g) || []).length;\n  check(\"skill deny occurrences\", count, 3);\n  checkTrue(\"keeper plugin wired\", (json.plugin || []).includes(\"./plugin/rtr-re-keeper.js\"), JSON.stringify(json.plugin));\n  checkTrue(\"no rtr-memory reference\", !raw.includes(\"rtr-memory\"));\n  check(\"memory MCP enabled\", json.mcp.memory.enabled, true);\n  checkTrue(\"memory MCP command\", JSON.stringify(json.mcp.memory.command).includes(\"rtr-re-mcp\"));\n}\n\n// acp.jsonc live + lab: todos-only protection\nfor (const p of [\"C:/Users/Saimono/.config/opencode/acp.jsonc\", \"C:/RTR/.opencode/acp.jsonc\"]) {\n  const { json } = loadJsonc(p);\n  check(\"protectedTools in \" + p, json.compress.protectedTools, [\"todowrite\"]);\n  check(\"acp enabled in \" + p, json.enabled, true);\n}\n\n// pyproject: registry shipped once per target (2 lines total, not 4)\n{\n  const t = fs.readFileSync(\"C:/RTR/pyproject.toml\", \"utf8\");\n  const lines = t.split(\"\\n\").filter((l) => l.includes(\"rtr_presets.json\"));\n  check(\"pyproject registry lines\", lines.length, 2);\n  checkTrue(\"sdist registry line\", lines.some((l) => l.includes('\"execution/rtr_presets.json\" = \"execution/rtr_presets.json\"')));\n}\n\n// sync guard: uv.lock shipped, locals preserved, locked-file safe, no phantom dir\n{\n  const t = t.includes('\".opencode/\"'));\n  checkTrue(\"sync preserves .cocoindex_code/\", t.includes('\".cocoindex_code/\"'));\n  checkTrue(\"sync warns on locked stale\", t.includes(\"WARN: could not remove stale\"));\n  checkTrue(\"no phantom tui-plugin dir\", !t.includes('\"tui-plugin\"'));\n}\n\n// python protection: todowrite-only in RTR + RE\nfor (const p of [\"C:/RTR/execution/context_budget.py\", checkTrue(\"old skill set gone in \" + p, !t.includes('frozenset({\"skill\"'));\n}\n\n// sync manifest: 312 files, uv.lock + keeper present, vendored absent\n{\n  const m = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(\"C:/RTR-RE/SYNC-MANIFEST.json\", \"utf8\"));\n  const paths = m.files.map((f) => f.path);\n  check(\"manifest count\", paths.length, 312);\n  checkTrue(\"manifest has p.startsWith(\"opencode-acp/\")));\n}\n\nconsole.log(fails === 0 ? \"VERIFY-CONFIG: ALL PASS\" : \"VERIFY-CONFIG: \" + fails + \" FAILURES\");\nprocess.exit(fails === 0 ? 0 : same fore RTR folder, but the ccc needs to be updated, since well i dont have to tell u this, just see the size difference of the .db inside cocoindex on RTR-RE VS RTR, to see that the Index succesfully catalogued the whole source code on RTR-RE whereas on RTR, it did not..
once this is done we will make a full catalogue without using GIT diff..
then Merge RTR-RE as standalone GIT.
only then we will RE-ADAPT the RTR to is original README.md form, updating naturally the information to the most update *rtr-role-type-retrieval 0.5.0*.
and then finally we will also update RTR-RE by also looking at the README.md corresponding, to is latest features and presets, also to 0.5.0, by also mitigating, what confounded us, that rtr itself is just the Retriever..
and RTR-RE is the Retriever + Core + Engine Beneath + Compressor + keeper..
its so easy!!
